1	# auxilio-extension
2	
3	Google **Chrome extension (MV3)** for Auxilio (Office Ops) — the **desktop** surface for registering office visitors directly inside Google Calendar. It is the richer counterpart to the `auxilio-workspace` Calendar add-on: where the add-on is mobile-capable but lives under the CardService "card ceiling," the extension gives a full React UI (realtime guest updates, per-guest invite toggle, template preview, inline edits, intentional auto-nudge).
4	
5	> Read the umbrella `../CLAUDE.md` for the four constraints and UX rules — they apply here.
6	> Strategy + approval analysis: `../Planning-docs/2026-06-18_calendar_visitor_chrome_extension_decision.md`.
7	> Build plan: the approved plan that scaffolded this repo.
8	
9	## What this is (and isn't)
10	
11	- **Is:** an MV3 extension (WXT + React 19 + TS) that injects a trigger into Google Calendar (web) and renders the registration flow in the **side panel**. A **channel surface / dumb adapter** — it forwards intent to `office-ops-engine`.
12	- **Isn't:** a place for business logic. Dedup, draft curation, invite/pass issuance, and lifecycle all live in `office-ops-engine`'s visitor plugin. This repo renders UI and calls the engine.
13	- **Isn't:** mobile. Extensions are desktop-Chrome only. The `auxilio-workspace` add-on remains the mobile surface. Both key off the same **magic address**.
14	
15	## Architecture — three MV3 surfaces
16	
17	- **`entrypoints/background.ts`** (service worker) — the only holder of tokens + network. Mints/caches the Google `id_token` (engine auth) + `access_token` (Calendar read), runs the Calendar API client and the engine `/addon/*` client, keeps per-tab event context, routes messages.
18	- **`entrypoints/calendar.content.ts`** (content script @ `calendar.google.com`) — a **dumb trigger/observer**: detect open event, decode `eid`, inject the "Register a visitor" button (Phase 1) and the magic-address banner (Phase 2). No tokens, no business logic.
19	- **`entrypoints/sidepanel/`** (the app) — React + TanStack Query UI. Pure presentation + server state.
20	
21	```
22	content script ──msg──> background ──HTTPS(id_token)──> office-ops-engine /api/visitor/calendar/addon/*
23	side panel    ──msg──> background ──Calendar API(access_token)──> canonical iCalUID + attendees
24	```
25	
26	## Hard rules
27	
28	1. **Calendar-only scopes. Never add Gmail/Drive.** Calendar is *sensitive* (verification, no paid audit). Gmail/Drive are *restricted* → annual CASA assessment. Hard line, same as the add-on.
29	2. **Engine owns the contract.** The extension calls the existing `/api/visitor/calendar/addon/{draft,preview,send,cancel-guest}` routes (ID-token authed). Do **not** add engine signature/scope changes from here. The only engine-side change is env: add this extension's OAuth client id to `AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` (audience allow-list).
30	3. **Background is the only secret/network holder.** Content script stays a dumb trigger; side panel stays presentation. Keep the thin-adapter boundary.
31	4. **Send the canonical `iCalUID`** (from the Calendar API `events.get`, not synthesized) so the extension path converges on the same `calendar_event_links` row as `.ics`/OAuth/add-on.
32	5. **Design: Material 3 tokens, not its code.** Our own `design/tokens.ts` + `global.css` (primary `#92288E`). No MUI/Tailwind/paper. **Icons: lucide-react only**, no Unicode glyphs/emoji in chrome.
33	6. **Animations subtle, only where needed** — CSS transitions driven by motion tokens; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.
34	7. **Internal-first.** Force-installed/unlisted to our Workspace → no OAuth verification yet. Public/commercial = a later packaging + verification exercise (CWS review + OAuth verification), per the decision doc.
35	
36	## Stack & commands
37	
38	WXT (MV3 over Vite) + React 19 + TypeScript + TanStack Query (server state) + Zustand (UI/session/event-context) + lucide-react.
39	
40	```bash
41	npm install
42	npm run dev        # WXT dev server; load .output/chrome-mv3 in chrome://extensions (Developer mode → Load unpacked)
43	npm run build      # production build → .output/chrome-mv3
44	npm run compile    # tsc --noEmit (type-check)
45	npm run zip        # packaged zip for store upload
46	```
47	
48	Env: copy `.env.example` → `.env` (engine URL, OAuth client id, magic address). `WXT_` vars are inlined at build.
49	
50	## Versioning
51	
52	**Bump `package.json` `version` on every change you ship to the browser.** WXT syncs it into `manifest.json` automatically, so `chrome://extensions` shows the loaded build's version. This is the check that a reload actually picked up your change — if the card's version doesn't match what you just built, the old bundle is still loaded (reload there and reopen the side panel). Use semver: patch for fixes, minor for features.
53	
54	## Deploy / distribution
55	
56	**Not Bifrost, not EAS.** Internal-first via Chrome admin **force-install** (unlisted, by extension id) or "Load unpacked" for dev. Public later via the Chrome Web Store (payments handled by our own engine; CWS no longer bills).
57	
```

1	/**
2	 * Content script on Google Calendar — the DOM layer of the hybrid model.
3	 *
4	 * It reads the open event surface (detail popover / expanded edit) to:
5	 *  - GATE the "Manage Visitors" button on the magic address being a guest,
6	 *  - capture the `data-eventid` (base64url "<eventId> <calendarId>"), and
7	 *  - take a best-effort SNAPSHOT (guest emails) so the panel can paint instantly
8	 *    and cover the unsaved (no-API) case.
9	 * The API layer (background) remains the authoritative source. We read only the
10	 * semantic `data-eventid`, the `[role="dialog"]` ARIA signal, and the surface's
11	 * visible text — no layout/CSS-class coupling, no injection into Google's markup.
12	 */
13	import { MAGIC_ADDRESS } from '@/lib/config';
14	import {
15	  ACTIVE_EID_KEY,
16	  ACTIVE_SNAPSHOT_KEY,
17	  AUTH_LAPSED,
18	  NUDGE_TARGETS,
19	  PANEL_STATE,
20	} from '@/lib/messaging';
21	import type { DomEventSnapshot, VisitorEventSummary } from '@/lib/types';
22	
23	/** True while our extension context is still valid. After an extension reload or
24	 *  update, this content script is orphaned: every `chrome.*` call then throws
25	 *  "Extension context invalidated" (synchronously — `.catch()` can't see it). We
26	 *  check this before touching any chrome API so an orphan stops quietly. */
27	const extAlive = (): boolean => {
28	  try {
29	    return !!chrome.runtime?.id;
30	  } catch {
31	    return false;
32	  }
33	};
34	
35	/** sendMessage that never throws or rejects, even once the context is gone. */
36	function safeSend<T = unknown>(message: unknown): Promise<T | undefined> {
37	  if (!extAlive()) return Promise.resolve(undefined);
38	  try {
39	    return Promise.resolve(chrome.runtime.sendMessage(message)).catch(() => undefined);
40	  } catch {
41	    return Promise.resolve(undefined);
42	  }
43	}
44	
45	/** storage.session helpers that no-op once the context is gone. */
46	function safeStorageGet(key: string): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
47	  if (!extAlive()) return Promise.resolve({});
48	  try {
49	    return Promise.resolve(chrome.storage.session.get(key)).catch(() => ({}));
50	  } catch {
51	    return Promise.resolve({});
52	  }
53	}
54	function safeStorageSet(items: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<void> {
55	  if (!extAlive()) return Promise.resolve();
56	  try {
57	    return Promise.resolve(chrome.storage.session.set(items)).catch(() => {});
58	  } catch {
59	    return Promise.resolve();
60	  }
61	}
62	
63	export default defineContentScript({
64	  matches: ['https://calendar.google.com/*'],
65	  main() {
66	    // The id of the most recently clicked event chip — the event whose popover
67	    // is opening. Reset when the surface closes so a later unrelated dialog
68	    // can't resurrect a stale button.
69	    let clickedEid: string | null = null;
70	    let lastSnapshot: DomEventSnapshot | null = null;
71	    let panelOpen = false;
72	    // The dialog node we've accepted as an event surface. Held so recognition is
73	    // sticky for the life of that node (see readSurface) — kills the button flicker.
74	    let eventDialog: HTMLElement | null = null;
75	    let followedEid: string | null = null; // last event auto-pushed to the panel
76	    let pendingMagic: { eid: string; eventId: string; title: string } | null = null;
77	    let everSawEventId = false;
78	
79	    // Button UI: a native injected button when a clean anchor exists, else a
80	    // floating fallback (Idea 2). The magic-address gate is dropped — the button
81	    // shows on any open event surface; clicking it IS the intent.
82	    const button = createButtonUI(() => {
83	      if (!lastSnapshot) return;
84	      // Always hand the click to the background as OPEN_FOR_EVENT. The
85	      // background is the single authority on whether the panel is connected
86	      // (it owns the panel's port): it calls sidePanel.open only when the panel
87	      // is closed, and otherwise just points the open panel at this event. We
88	      // deliberately do NOT branch on a local panelOpen/opening flag here — those
89	      // drift out of sync (a missed PANEL_STATE, or a failed open leaving a latch
90	      // stuck) and were the reason a click sometimes silently did nothing.
91	      openFor(lastSnapshot);
92	      // Note: we intentionally do NOT navigate the tab to the event here. The
93	      // panel resolves the event itself (Calendar API), so reloading the page
94	      // adds nothing but cost + surprise. "Open in Calendar" in the panel is the
95	      // explicit, user-initiated way to jump to the event.
96	    });
97	
98	    // In-page nudge banner: visitor events the user saved but hasn't acted on.
99	    // Driven by the background sync (robust), not live DOM scanning.
100	    const nudge = mountNudge();
101	    safeSend<{ ok?: boolean; data?: VisitorEventSummary[] }>({ type: 'GET_NUDGE_TARGETS' }).then(
102	      (res) => {
103	        if (res?.ok) nudge.setTargets(res.data as VisitorEventSummary[]);
104	      },
105	    );
106	
107	    // Reconnect banner: shown when the background reports a recoverable auth lapse
108	    // (the user was connected but a silent token renew failed), so nudging never
109	    // dies silently. The background broadcasts AUTH_LAPSED on each sync.
110	    const reconnect = mountReconnect(() => safeSend({ type: 'OPEN_PANEL' }));
111	
112	    // Seed the panel-open state on (re)load so auto-follow works after a
113	    // same-tab navigation (the panel persists; this content script is fresh).
114	    safeSend<{ ok?: boolean; data?: { open: boolean } }>({ type: 'GET_PANEL_STATE' }).then((res) => {
115	      if (res?.ok) {
116	        panelOpen = !!res.data?.open;
117	        nudge.setPanelOpen(panelOpen);
118	        reconnect.setPanelOpen(panelOpen);
119	        render();
120	      }
121	    });
122	
123	    // Fast detection: sync on load, on tab focus, and right after leaving the
124	    // event editor (a likely save) — so the banner appears in seconds, not up
125	    // to a minute, and shows immediately when the user returns to the tab.
126	    let lastSync = 0;
127	    const syncNow = () => {
128	      const now = Date.now();
129	      if (now - lastSync < 4000) return; // throttle
130	      lastSync = now;
131	      safeSend({ type: 'SYNC_NOW' });
132	    };
133	    // Burst: fire several syncs after a likely save to beat Google's API
134	    // eventual-consistency lag (a fresh events.list may not show the just-saved
135	    // event for a few seconds).
136	    let lastBurst = 0;
137	    const syncBurst = () => {
138	      const now = Date.now();
139	      if (now - lastBurst < 2000) return;
140	      lastBurst = now;
141	      for (const d of [0, 3000, 8000]) {
142	        setTimeout(() => safeSend({ type: 'SYNC_NOW' }), d);
143	      }
144	    };
145	    syncNow();
146	    document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => {
147	      if (document.visibilityState === 'visible') syncNow();
148	    });
149	    window.addEventListener('focus', syncNow);
150	    let wasEditor = /\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname);
151	    const editorPoll = setInterval(() => {
152	      if (!extAlive()) return teardown(); // orphaned by an extension reload → stop
153	      const isEditor = /\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname);
154	      if (wasEditor && !isEditor) syncBurst(); // left the editor → likely saved
155	      wasEditor = isEditor;
156	      // Safety net: re-assert the button. The MutationObserver is debounced, so a
157	      // continuous scroll (which fires mutations faster than the debounce) can
158	      // starve it and never re-run render after Google re-renders the modal and
159	      // drops our injected button. This guarantees it comes back within ~1s.
160	      render();
161	    }, 1000);
162	
163	    // Stop all work once our context is invalidated (extension reloaded/updated),
164	    // so an orphaned script doesn't keep observing/polling — and remove our now-
165	    // dead button (its click can't reach the background) instead of leaving a
166	    // zombie that looks live but does nothing.
167	    let disposed = false;
168	    function teardown() {
169	      if (disposed) return;
170	      disposed = true;
171	      clearInterval(editorPoll);
172	      observer.disconnect();
173	      button.update(false);
174	    }
175	
176	    /** Read the open event surface, or null if none is open. `eid` may be '' for
177	     *  a brand-new, not-yet-saved event (no data-eventid / URL eid yet). */
178	    function readSurface(): { el: HTMLElement; eid: string } | null {
179	      const fromUrl = urlEid();
180	      const dialog = document.querySelector('[role="dialog"]') as HTMLElement | null;
181	      // A dialog that isn't an event surface — e.g. Google's "Send update emails
182	      // to existing guests?" save confirmation — must NEVER host our button, and
183	      // while one is open we suppress entirely so we don't inject onto it (or
184	      // behind it, over the editor). This is what kept the button appearing on
185	      // unrelated dialogs and "flashing" away when such a dialog opened/closed.
186	      if (dialog) {
187	        // Sticky recognition: once a dialog node is accepted as an event
188	        // surface, keep treating it as one until it actually leaves the DOM.
189	        // Google re-renders the popover's innards constantly; isEventDialog is a
190	        // heuristic, so a single tick where it momentarily reads false would
191	        // otherwise yank the button and pop it back a frame later — the flicker.
192	        // A genuinely different dialog (a confirmation) is a different node, so
193	        // it's still evaluated strictly and correctly rejected.
194	        const isEvent = dialog === eventDialog || isEventDialog(dialog);
195	        if (!isEvent) return null;
196	        eventDialog = dialog;
197	      } else {
198	        eventDialog = null;
199	      }
200	      if (fromUrl) {
201	        return { el: dialog ?? document.body, eid: fromUrl };
202	      }
203	      // Full-screen create/edit editor. An existing event always carries its eid
204	      // in the URL (handled above), so reaching here means a brand-new, unsaved
205	      // event → eid ''. Never fall back to clickedEid: it can be stale from a
206	      // previously viewed event and would point the panel at the wrong one.
207	      if (/\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {
208	        return { el: dialog ?? document.body, eid: '' };
209	      }
210	      // Detail / quick-create popover.
211	      if (dialog) return { el: dialog, eid: clickedEid ?? '' };
212	      return null;
213	    }
214	
215	    function snapshotOf(el: HTMLElement, eid: string): DomEventSnapshot {
216	      const text = el.innerText || '';
217	      const lower = text.toLowerCase();
218	      const magicPresent = !!MAGIC_ADDRESS && lower.includes(MAGIC_ADDRESS);
219	      const guestEmails = extractEmails(text).filter(
220	        (e) => e.toLowerCase() !== MAGIC_ADDRESS,
221	      );
222	      // Best-effort title (cosmetic — the API sync replaces it). The full editor
223	      // exposes the title as a text input; the detail popover (the common case)
224	      // shows it as a heading, not an input — so fall back to the open dialog's
225	      // heading / accessible name. Scoped to the dialog so we never grab the
226	      // page's own H1 ("Calendar") when no event surface is open.
227	      const titleInput = el.querySelector(
228	        'input[aria-label*="title" i], input[placeholder*="title" i]',
229	      ) as HTMLInputElement | null;
230	      const dialog = el.matches?.('[role="dialog"]')
231	        ? el
232	        : el.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[role="dialog"]');
233	      const heading = dialog?.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[role="heading"], h1, h2');
234	      const title =
235	        titleInput?.value?.trim() ||
236	        heading?.innerText?.trim() ||
237	        dialog?.getAttribute('aria-label')?.trim() ||
238	        undefined;
239	      return { eid, magicPresent, guestEmails, title };
240	    }
241	
242	    function render() {
243	      if (!extAlive()) return teardown(); // orphaned by an extension reload → stop
244	      const surface = readSurface();
245	      lastSnapshot = surface ? snapshotOf(surface.el, surface.eid) : null;
246	
247	      // Instant nudge: while viewing a visitor event WITH a real eid, remember
248	      // it; when the user then leaves that surface (closed popover / saved &
249	      // left editor), show the nudge for it IMMEDIATELY — no API round-trip.
250	      if (lastSnapshot?.magicPresent && lastSnapshot.eid) {
251	        pendingMagic = {
252	          eid: lastSnapshot.eid,
253	          eventId: decodeEventId(lastSnapshot.eid),
254	          title: lastSnapshot.title ?? '',
255	        };
256	      } else if (!surface && pendingMagic) {
257	        nudge.addOptimistic({
258	          eid: pendingMagic.eid,
259	          eventId: pendingMagic.eventId,
260	          iCalUid: '',
261	          title: pendingMagic.title,
262	          start: undefined,
263	        });
264	        syncBurst(); // confirm/refresh from the API right after
265	        pendingMagic = null;
266	      }
267	
268	      // Show the button on any open event surface — including while the panel is
269	      // open. We keep the floating fallback available even then: a missing button
270	      // reads as broken (the user's report), and the native in-popover button can
271	      // lose the first-open injection race. The grace period in update() still
272	      // lets the native button win whenever it injects in time, so the FAB only
273	      // appears as a genuine last resort. (We no longer navigate the tab on click,
274	      // so the old "flash while a navigated page loads" concern is moot.)
275	      button.update(!!surface);
276	      if (panelOpen) maybeFollow();
277	    }
278	
279	    /** Push the open *saved* event to the panel so it follows what you're viewing.
280	     *  Saved only (non-empty eid); deduped; the panel guards against switching
281	     *  mid-action. */
282	    function maybeFollow() {
283	      const snap = lastSnapshot;
284	      if (!snap || !snap.eid) return; // new/unsaved events don't auto-switch
285	      if (snap.eid === followedEid) return;
286	      followedEid = snap.eid;
287	      safeSend({ type: 'FOLLOW_EVENT', eid: snap.eid, snapshot: snap });
288	    }
289	
290	    document.addEventListener(
291	      'click',
292	      (e) => {
293	        const chip = (e.target as Element | null)?.closest?.('[data-eventid]');
294	        const id = chip?.getAttribute('data-eventid');
295	        if (id) {
296	          everSawEventId = true;
297	          clickedEid = id;
298	        }
299	        // Re-check a few times: Google paints the popover progressively, so a
300	        // single 60ms probe can land before the surface (or its rows) exist,
301	        // and we'd then wait up to a second for the safety-net poll. Staggered
302	        // probes catch a slow paint quickly; render() is idempotent + cheap.
303	        for (const d of [60, 250, 600]) setTimeout(render, d);
304	      },
305	      true,
306	    );
307	
308	    document.addEventListener(
309	      'keydown',
310	      (e) => {
311	        if (e.key === 'Escape') {
312	          clickedEid = null;
313	          setTimeout(render, 60);
314	        }
315	      },
316	      true,
317	    );
318	
319	    // Detect surface open/close + edits (e.g. adding the magic address live).
320	    let timer: number | undefined;
321	    const observer = new MutationObserver(() => {
322	      clearTimeout(timer);
323	      timer = setTimeout(() => {
324	        if (!document.querySelector('[role="dialog"]') && !urlEid()) {
325	          clickedEid = null;
326	        }
327	        render();
328	      }, 150) as unknown as number;
329	    });
330	    observer.observe(document.body, { childList: true, subtree: true });
331	
332	    window.addEventListener('popstate', render);
333	
334	    // Hide the in-page button while the side panel is open.
335	    chrome.runtime.onMessage.addListener(
336	      (msg: {
337	        type?: string;
338	        open?: boolean;
339	        targets?: VisitorEventSummary[];
340	        cancelled?: string[];
341	        lapsed?: boolean;
342	      }) => {
343	        if (msg?.type === PANEL_STATE) {
344	          panelOpen = !!msg.open;
345	          if (!panelOpen) followedEid = null; // re-follow next time it opens
346	          nudge.setPanelOpen(panelOpen); // banner hides while the panel is open
347	          reconnect.setPanelOpen(panelOpen);
348	          render();
349	        } else if (msg?.type === NUDGE_TARGETS) {
350	          nudge.setTargets(msg.targets ?? [], msg.cancelled ?? []);
351	        } else if (msg?.type === AUTH_LAPSED) {
352	          reconnect.setLapsed(!!msg.lapsed);
353	        }
354	      },
355	    );
356	
357	    setTimeout(() => {
358	      if (!everSawEventId && !document.querySelector('[data-eventid]')) {
359	        console.warn(
360	          '[auxilio] no [data-eventid] found — click detection may be unavailable; use the panel’s event list.',
361	        );
362	      }
363	    }, 90_000);
364	
365	    render();
366	    console.log('[auxilio] content script ready on Google Calendar');
367	  },
368	});
369	
370	function openFor(snapshot: DomEventSnapshot) {
371	  return safeSend({ type: 'OPEN_FOR_EVENT', eid: snapshot.eid, snapshot });
372	}
373	
374	/** Event id from the expanded-edit URL path, if present. */
375	function urlEid(): string | null {
376	  const path = location.pathname.match(/\/eventedit\/([^/?#]+)/);
377	  if (path && path[1] !== 'eventedit') return safeDecode(path[1]);
378	  const q = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get('eid');
379	  return q ? safeDecode(q) : null;
380	}
381	
382	/** Pull unique email-looking strings from text (best-effort guest scrape). */
383	function extractEmails(text: string): string[] {
384	  const matches = text.match(/[a-z0-9._%+-]+@[a-z0-9.-]+\.[a-z]{2,}/gi) ?? [];
385	  return [...new Set(matches.map((m) => m.toLowerCase()))];
386	}
387	
388	/** eid (base64url of "<eventId> <calendarId>") → eventId, for nudge dedup. */
389	function decodeEventId(eid: string): string {
390	  try {
391	    let b64 = eid.replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/');
392	    b64 += '='.repeat((4 - (b64.length % 4)) % 4);
393	    return atob(b64).split(' ')[0] || '';
394	  } catch {
395	    return '';
396	  }
397	}
398	
399	function safeDecode(s: string): string {
400	  try {
401	    return decodeURIComponent(s);
402	  } catch {
403	    return s;
404	  }
405	}
406	
407	const USERS_SVG = `
408	<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="20" height="20" viewBox="0 0 24 24"
409	  fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round">
410	  <path d="M16 21v-2a4 4 0 0 0-4-4H6a4 4 0 0 0-4 4v2"/><circle cx="9" cy="7" r="4"/>
411	  <path d="M22 21v-2a4 4 0 0 0-3-3.87"/><path d="M16 3.13a4 4 0 0 1 0 7.75"/></svg>`;
412	
413	const LABEL = 'Manage Visitors';
414	const INJECT_ID = 'auxilio-manage-visitors';
415	
416	/**
417	 * Button UI manager: injects a native-styled "Manage Visitors" button as a real,
418	 * full-width row in the surface — below the Availability/Visibility section on the
419	 * edit page, at the bottom of the outermost content list in the detail popover (so
420	 * it scrolls with the rest of the UI, never an overlay) — and falls back to a
421	 * floating button only when no anchor is found. `update(show)` decides per render.
422	 */
423	function createButtonUI(onClick: () => void) {
424	  const fab = mountFloating(onClick);
425	  let injected: HTMLElement | null = null;
426	  let retryTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
427	
428	  // A clean injection anchor: where to drop the button + how far to indent it so
429	  // it lines up with the section's content column (past Google's icon gutter).
430	  // `inline` = sit next to a native button instead of as its own row.
431	  type Anchor = { insert: (el: HTMLElement) => void; inset: number; inline?: boolean };
432	
433	  /** Text/ARIA-based only — never CSS classes — so we stay decoupled from
434	   *  Google's obfuscated markup. Places the button where it reads as part of the
435	   *  form: between Description and the next section on the edit page, and as the
436	   *  last row of the detail popover's content list. Floating only as last resort. */
437	  function findAnchor(): Anchor | null {
438	    // Full-screen edit page → our own row just BELOW the Availability/Visibility
439	    // info section (falling back to above Description).
440	    if (/\/eventedit/.test(location.pathname)) {
441	      const avail = findAvailabilityInfo();
442	      const afterAvail = avail ? rowsListFor(avail) : null;
443	      if (afterAvail) {
444	        const { list, row } = afterAvail;
445	        return { insert: (el) => list.insertBefore(el, row.nextSibling), inset: rowInset(row, list) };
446	      }
447	      const desc = findEditDescription();
448	      const aboveDesc = desc ? rowsListFor(desc) : null;
449	      if (aboveDesc) {
450	        const { list, row } = aboveDesc;
451	        return { insert: (el) => list.insertBefore(el, row), inset: rowInset(row, list) };
452	      }
453	      // Fallback within the edit page: inline, right before the native "Save".
454	      const save = findButtonByText('save');
455	      const parent = save?.parentElement;
456	      if (save && parent) return { insert: (el) => parent.insertBefore(el, save), inset: 0, inline: true };
457	      return null;
458	    }
459	    // Detail popover / modal → BOTTOM of the OUTERMOST content list (after every
460	    // section, including the guest list). Robust whether or not guests exist.
461	    const dialog = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[role="dialog"]');
462	    if (dialog) {
463	      const list = findContentList(dialog);
464	      if (list) {
465	        const ref = firstIconLedRow(list);
466	        return { insert: (el) => list.appendChild(el), inset: ref ? rowInset(ref, list) : 0 };
467	      }
468	      // Fallback → bottom of the scrolling content.
469	      const scope = scrollContainer(dialog) ?? dialog;
470	      const l = mainVerticalList(scope) ?? scope;
471	      return { insert: (el) => l.appendChild(el), inset: 0 };
472	    }
473	    return null;
474	  }
475	
476	  function ensureInjected(): boolean {
477	    if (injected && injected.isConnected) return true; // still placed — skip the scan
478	    const anchor = findAnchor();
479	    if (!anchor) return false;
480	    removeInjected();
481	    ensureInjectedStyles();
482	    injected = anchor.inline
483	      ? buildInlineButton(onClick)
484	      : buildInjectedRow(onClick, anchor.inset);
485	    anchor.insert(injected);
486	    return true;
487	  }
488	
489	  function removeInjected() {
490	    injected?.remove();
491	    injected = null;
492	  }
493	
494	  return {
495	    // `allowFab` gates the floating fallback. It's only there to surface the
496	    // panel when we can't inject a native button; with the panel already open
497	    // it's redundant (auto-follow handles the viewed event), and it would flash
498	    // at the corner while a navigated page is still loading. So callers pass
499	    // false when the panel is open.
500	    update(show: boolean, allowFab = true) {
501	      clearTimeout(retryTimer);
502	      if (!show) {
503	        removeInjected();
504	        fab.setVisible(false);
505	        return;
506	      }
507	      // Try to inject now; if the anchor isn't in the DOM / laid out yet (Google
508	      // is still rendering the surface), keep retrying for a few seconds. The
509	      // MutationObserver covers DOM changes, but layout can settle — giving
510	      // elements their width — without a childList mutation, so width-based
511	      // anchor checks can miss that window. This retry guarantees we don't.
512	      let attempts = 0;
513	      const attempt = () => {
514	        const placed = ensureInjected();
515	        // Show the floating fallback only after a short grace period, so on a
516	        // normally-rendering surface the native button just appears — no flash
517	        // of the FAB getting replaced a moment later.
518	        fab.setVisible(allowFab && !placed && attempts >= 3);
519	        if (placed || attempts++ >= 30) return;
520	        retryTimer = setTimeout(attempt, 150);
521	      };
522	      attempt();
523	    },
524	  };
525	}
526	
527	/**
528	 * Is this open `[role="dialog"]` an actual event surface (detail popover / quick
529	 * edit), rather than one of Google's transient confirmation dialogs — e.g.
530	 * "Send update emails to existing Google Calendar guests?" or "Delete this
531	 * event?" — which we must not decorate? Positive, stable signals only: an event
532	 * chip id, the magic address in its text, or several icon-led section rows
533	 * (time / guests / location). A confirmation dialog is just a question plus a
534	 * row of action buttons, so it matches none of these.
535	 */
536	function isEventDialog(dialog: HTMLElement): boolean {
537	  if (dialog.querySelector('[data-eventid]')) return true;
538	  const text = (dialog.innerText || '').toLowerCase();
539	  if (MAGIC_ADDRESS && text.includes(MAGIC_ADDRESS)) return true;
540	  return !!findContentList(dialog);
541	}
542	
543	/** Find a clickable element whose visible text matches (case-insensitive). */
544	function findButtonByText(text: string): HTMLElement | null {
545	  const wanted = text.toLowerCase();
546	  const els = document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('button,[role="button"]');
547	  for (const el of els) {
548	    if ((el.textContent ?? '').trim().toLowerCase() === wanted) return el;
549	  }
550	  return null;
551	}
552	
553	const isVisible = (el: HTMLElement) => el.offsetParent !== null || el.getClientRects().length > 0;
554	
555	/** The Description editor on the event edit page. ARIA/placeholder text only —
556	 *  never CSS classes (Google's are obfuscated and churn). */
557	function findEditDescription(): HTMLElement | null {
558	  const els = document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(
559	    '[aria-label*="description" i], [placeholder*="description" i]',
560	  );
561	  for (const el of els) if (isVisible(el)) return el;
562	  return null;
563	}
564	
565	/** The Availability/Visibility info block on the edit page. Prefers Google's
566	 *  stable `jsname` hook, falling back to the info text. */
567	function findAvailabilityInfo(): HTMLElement | null {
568	  const byJsname = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[jsname="g7cnnb"]');
569	  if (byJsname && isVisible(byJsname)) return byJsname;
570	  for (const el of document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('span,div')) {
571	    const t = (el.textContent ?? '').trim().toLowerCase();
572	    if (t.startsWith('availability might be shown') && isVisible(el)) return el;
573	  }
574	  return null;
575	}
576	
577	/** The OUTERMOST rows list inside `root`: the shallowest container whose own
578	 *  children include several icon-led section rows. Breadth-first so a nested
579	 *  guest list never wins over the main content list. */
580	function findContentList(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
581	  const queue: HTMLElement[] = [];
582	  for (const c of root.children) if (c instanceof HTMLElement) queue.push(c);
583	  while (queue.length) {
584	    const el = queue.shift()!;
585	    if (!isVisible(el)) continue;
586	    if (iconLedRowCount(el) >= 2) return el;
587	    for (const c of el.children) if (c instanceof HTMLElement) queue.push(c);
588	  }
589	  return null;
590	}
591	
592	/** The first wide icon-led row in a list — a reference for measuring the text
593	 *  column inset. */
594	function firstIconLedRow(list: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
595	  const lw = list.getBoundingClientRect().width;
596	  for (const c of list.children) {
597	    if (!(c instanceof HTMLElement) || !isVisible(c) || !looksLikeRow(c)) continue;
598	    if (lw > 0 && c.getBoundingClientRect().width < lw * 0.5) continue;
599	    return c;
600	  }
601	  return null;
602	}
603	
604	/** Does this element stack its visible children vertically (a column of rows),
605	 *  rather than laying them out side by side? Geometry, not CSS classes. */
606	function stacksVertically(el: HTMLElement): boolean {
607	  const kids = [...el.children].filter((c): c is HTMLElement => c instanceof HTMLElement && isVisible(c));
608	  if (kids.length < 2) return false;
609	  for (let i = 1; i < kids.length; i++) {
610	    const a = kids[i - 1].getBoundingClientRect();
611	    const b = kids[i].getBoundingClientRect();
612	    if (b.top >= a.bottom - 2) return true; // the next child starts below the previous
613	  }
614	  return false;
615	}
616	
617	/** A native Calendar content row = a block whose first child is a decorative
618	 *  (aria-hidden) leading icon, followed by content. This is the one stable,
619	 *  class-free signal Google keeps across both the popover and the edit page. */
620	function looksLikeRow(el: HTMLElement): boolean {
621	  const first = el.firstElementChild;
622	  return (
623	    !!first &&
624	    el.childElementCount >= 2 &&
625	    (first.getAttribute('aria-hidden') === 'true' ||
626	      !!first.querySelector('svg, img, i.google-material-icons'))
627	  );
628	}
629	
630	/** How many of a container's visible children are icon-led *section* rows.
631	 *  Rows must span most of the container's width, which excludes narrow icon
632	 *  clusters like a description formatting toolbar. */
633	function iconLedRowCount(container: HTMLElement): number {
634	  const cw = container.getBoundingClientRect().width;
635	  let n = 0;
636	  for (const c of container.children) {
637	    if (!(c instanceof HTMLElement) || !isVisible(c) || !looksLikeRow(c)) continue;
638	    if (cw > 0 && c.getBoundingClientRect().width < cw * 0.5) continue; // skip toolbars/chips
639	    n++;
640	  }
641	  return n;
642	}
643	
644	/** From a field/label, climb to the rows list that holds it: the first ancestor
645	 *  whose parent contains several icon-led rows. Returns that list plus the row
646	 *  (a direct child of the list) that contains `el` — so we can insert relative
647	 *  to it. Null if no rows list is recognised. */
648	function rowsListFor(el: HTMLElement): { list: HTMLElement; row: HTMLElement } | null {
649	  let node: HTMLElement = el;
650	  while (node.parentElement && node.parentElement !== document.body) {
651	    const parent = node.parentElement;
652	    if (iconLedRowCount(parent) >= 2) return { list: parent, row: node };
653	    node = parent;
654	  }
655	  return null;
656	}
657	
658	/** The biggest vertically-stacking container inside `root` — the content column
659	 *  whose last child is the visual bottom of the surface. */
660	function mainVerticalList(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
661	  let best: HTMLElement | null = null;
662	  let bestArea = 0;
663	  for (const el of root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('*')) {
664	    if (!isVisible(el) || !stacksVertically(el)) continue;
665	    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
666	    const area = r.width * r.height;
667	    if (area > bestArea) {
668	      bestArea = area;
669	      best = el;
670	    }
671	  }
672	  return best;
673	}
674	
675	/** Left indent (px) that lines our button up with a native row's *text* column,
676	 *  past Google's leading icon gutter. Measured from where the row's first text
677	 *  actually paints, relative to the list we insert into. */
678	function rowInset(row: HTMLElement, list: HTMLElement): number {
679	  const gap = firstTextLeft(row) - list.getBoundingClientRect().left;
680	  return Number.isFinite(gap) && gap > 0 && gap < 240 ? Math.round(gap) : 0;
681	}
682	
683	/** Left edge of the first painted text inside `el` (skips the decorative icon
684	 *  gutter), or the element's own left if it has none. */
685	function firstTextLeft(el: HTMLElement): number {
686	  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(el, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
687	  let node: Node | null;
688	  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {
689	    if (!node.textContent || !node.textContent.trim()) continue;
690	    const range = document.createRange();
691	    range.selectNodeContents(node);
692	    const rect = range.getBoundingClientRect();
693	    if (rect.width > 0) return rect.left;
694	  }
695	  return el.getBoundingClientRect().left;
696	}
697	
698	/** The scrolling content region inside a surface (overflow-y auto/scroll),
699	 *  largest first — so our row lives with the content and scrolls, not in a
700	 *  pinned header/footer. Null when nothing scrolls. */
701	function scrollContainer(root: HTMLElement): HTMLElement | null {
702	  let best: HTMLElement | null = null;
703	  let bestArea = 0;
704	  for (const el of root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('*')) {
705	    if (!isVisible(el)) continue;
706	    const oy = getComputedStyle(el).overflowY;
707	    if (oy !== 'auto' && oy !== 'scroll') continue;
708	    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
709	    const area = r.width * r.height;
710	    if (area > bestArea) {
711	      bestArea = area;
712	      best = el;
713	    }
714	  }
715	  return best;
716	}
717	
718	/** Stop our interactions dismissing Google's modal / triggering its buttons. */
719	function shieldInteractions(el: HTMLElement) {
720	  for (const type of ['pointerdown', 'mousedown', 'touchstart'] as const) {
721	    el.addEventListener(
722	      type,
723	      (e) => {
724	        e.stopPropagation();
725	        e.preventDefault(); // don't move focus out of the Calendar dialog
726	      },
727	      true,
728	    );
729	  }
730	  el.addEventListener('click', (e) => e.stopPropagation(), true);
731	}
732	
733	const STYLE_ID = 'auxilio-manage-visitors-style';
734	
735	/** One-time stylesheet for the injected (light-DOM) button. Styled to match a
736	 *  native Material outlined Calendar button — same shape, size, typography and
737	 *  hover state-layer — so it reads as part of Google's UI, just brand-tinted. */
738	function ensureInjectedStyles() {
739	  if (document.getElementById(STYLE_ID)) return;
740	  const s = document.createElement('style');
741	  s.id = STYLE_ID;
742	  s.textContent = `
743	    .auxilio-mv-row{box-sizing:border-box;display:flex;align-items:center;width:100%;padding:8px 0;}
744	    .auxilio-mv-btn{
745	      box-sizing:border-box;display:inline-flex;align-items:center;gap:8px;
746	      font-family:'Google Sans','Roboto',Arial,sans-serif;font-size:14px;font-weight:500;
747	      line-height:20px;letter-spacing:.25px;color:#92288e;background:transparent;
748	      border:1px solid #747775;border-radius:999px;padding:8px 18px 8px 14px;
749	      min-height:40px;cursor:pointer;white-space:nowrap;
750	      transition:background-color 120ms ease,border-color 120ms ease;
751	    }
752	    .auxilio-mv-btn:hover{background:rgba(146,40,142,.08);}
753	    .auxilio-mv-btn:active{background:rgba(146,40,142,.12);}
754	    .auxilio-mv-btn:focus-visible{outline:2px solid #92288e;outline-offset:2px;}
755	    .auxilio-mv-btn[disabled]{opacity:.6;cursor:default;}
756	    .auxilio-mv-btn svg{width:18px;height:18px;flex:0 0 auto;}
757	  `;
758	  document.documentElement.appendChild(s);
759	}

761	/** The native-styled button itself (light DOM, classes from ensureInjectedStyles). */

772	/** A full-width row carrying the button, indented to align with the section
773	 *  content column. Flows like a native section, so it can't overlap siblings. */
774	function buildInjectedRow(onClick: () => void, inset: number): HTMLElement {
775	  const row = document.createElement('div');
776	  row.id = INJECT_ID;

778	  if (inset) row.style.paddingLeft = `${inset}px`;
779	  row.appendChild(makeNativeButton(onClick));
780	  // Guard only the row's empty padding — clicks there must not dismiss Google's
781	  // surface; the button manages its own interactions (shieldInteractions).
782	  shieldPadding(row);

791	  return btn;

793	
794	/** Stop interactions on the row's own padding (but not the button) from reaching
795	 *  Google — so an accidental tap beside the button can't close the dialog. */
796	function shieldPadding(row: HTMLElement) {
797	  const guard = (e: Event) => {
798	    if (e.target === row) {
799	      e.preventDefault();
800	      e.stopPropagation();
801	    }
802	  };
803	  for (const type of ['pointerdown', 'mousedown', 'touchstart', 'click'] as const) {
804	    row.addEventListener(type, guard, true);
805	  }
806	}

814	  // swallow clicks for) Google's own bottom drawer buttons (e.g. the event-edit add-on

816	  host.style.cssText = 'position:fixed;right:24px;bottom:24px;z-index:2147483646;pointer-events:none;';

847	/** Shared activate handler (pointerup + click, double-fire-guarded). */
848	function wireActivate(btn: HTMLButtonElement, onClick: () => void) {

912	      .manage:hover{box-shadow:0 2px 6px rgba(0,0,0,.2);}

932	  let targets: VisitorEventSummary[] = [];

956	  /** Merged, deduped, soonest-first (synced target wins over optimistic). */

1	import { useEffect, useState } from 'react';
2	import { ChevronDown, Phone, User } from 'lucide-react';
3	import { Chip, Switch, TextField } from '@/design/components';
4	import type { DraftGuest } from '@/lib/types';
5	
6	type GuestEdit = {
7	  include?: boolean;
8	  name?: string;
9	  phone?: string;
10	};
11	
12	/** Two-letter monogram from a name ("Jane Doe" → "JD") or an email local-part
13	 *  ("jane.doe@x" → "JD"), so each guest has a stable, recognisable avatar. */
14	function monogram(seed: string): string {
15	  const parts = seed.trim().split(/[\s._-]+/).filter(Boolean);
16	  if (parts.length === 0) return '?';
17	  if (parts.length === 1) return parts[0].slice(0, 2).toUpperCase();
18	  return (parts[0][0] + parts[1][0]).toUpperCase();
19	}
20	
21	export function RosterRow({
22	  guest,
23	  onChange,
24	}: {
25	  guest: DraftGuest;
26	  onChange: (edit: GuestEdit) => void;
27	}) {
28	  const [name, setName] = useState(guest.name);
29	  const [phone, setPhone] = useState(guest.phone ?? '');
30	  // Details stay collapsed by default so each card is compact; the host opens
31	  // them only to make a correction. Sent guests are editable too — re-sending
32	  // pushes the change onto the already-issued pass.
33	  const [editing, setEditing] = useState(false);
34	
35	  // Keep local inputs in sync when the server reconciles the row.
36	  useEffect(() => setName(guest.name), [guest.name]);
37	  useEffect(() => setPhone(guest.phone ?? ''), [guest.phone]);
38	
39	  const sent = guest.status === 'sent';
40	  const cancelled = guest.status === 'cancelled';
41	  const display = guest.name || guest.email;
42	  // A sent guest toggled OFF is a pending cancel — re-sending will revoke their
43	  // pass (engine applyDraft cancels !include guests with an active pass), so we
44	  // dim the row and don't offer edits. Everyone we'd still issue/update a pass
45	  // for can be corrected, whether or not their pass already went out.
46	  const pendingCancel = sent && !guest.include;
47	  const editable = guest.include && !cancelled;
48	
49	  // Switch is no longer locked once a pass is sent: toggling off cancels on the
50	  // next update (mirrors the add-on), toggling back on re-issues.
51	  const switchLabel = sent
52	    ? guest.include
53	      ? `Cancel the pass for ${guest.email}`
54	      : `Re-issue a pass for ${guest.email}`
55	    : guest.include
56	      ? `Don’t invite ${guest.email}`
57	      : `Invite ${guest.email}`;
58	
59	  return (
60	    <div className={`guest${guest.include ? '' : ' guest--dim'}`}>
61	      <div className="guest__head">
62	        <span
63	          className={
64	            'guest__avatar' +
65	            (pendingCancel
66	              ? ' guest__avatar--dim'
67	              : sent
68	                ? ' guest__avatar--sent'
69	                : guest.include
70	                  ? ''
71	                  : ' guest__avatar--dim')
72	          }
73	          aria-hidden
74	        >
75	          {monogram(guest.name || guest.email.split('@')[0])}
76	        </span>
77	
78	        <div className="guest__id">
79	          <div className="guest__name">
80	            <span className="type-label row__ellipsis">{display}</span>
81	            {guest.internal && !sent && !cancelled && <Chip>Internal</Chip>}
82	            {sent && guest.include && <Chip tone="success">Pass sent</Chip>}
83	            {pendingCancel && <Chip tone="error">Will cancel</Chip>}
84	            {cancelled && <Chip tone="error">Cancelled</Chip>}
85	          </div>
86	          <div className="type-label-sm text-muted row__ellipsis">{guest.email}</div>
87	        </div>
88	
89	        <Switch
90	          checked={guest.include}
91	          onChange={(v) => onChange({ include: v })}
92	          label={switchLabel}
93	        />
94	      </div>
95	
96	      {editable ? (
97	        // Full-bleed "scroll" drawer pinned to the card's bottom edge: a subtle,
98	        // edge-to-edge handle the host pulls open only when they need to correct
99	        // a detail — keeps each card compact by default.
100	        <div className={`guest__drawer${editing ? ' guest__drawer--open' : ''}`}>
101	          <button
102	            type="button"
103	            className="guest__handle"
104	            aria-expanded={editing}

106	          >
107	            <ChevronDown size={16} strokeWidth={2} className="guest__chev" />
108	            {editing ? 'Hide details' : guest.phone ? 'Edit details' : 'Add phone & name'}
109	          </button>
110	          {editing && (
111	            <div className="guest__form">
112	              <TextField
113	                label="Visitor name"
114	                value={name}
115	                leadingIcon={<User size={16} strokeWidth={2} />}
116	                onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)}
117	                onBlur={() => name !== guest.name && onChange({ name })}
118	                placeholder="Full name"
119	              />
120	              <TextField
121	                label="Phone (for WhatsApp pass)"
122	                value={phone}
123	                inputMode="tel"
124	                leadingIcon={<Phone size={16} strokeWidth={2} />}
125	                onChange={(e) => setPhone(e.target.value)}
126	                onBlur={() => phone !== (guest.phone ?? '') && onChange({ phone })}
127	                placeholder="+91…"
128	              />
129	              {sent && (
130	                <div className="type-label-sm text-muted">
131	                  Re-send to push this change onto the pass already issued.
132	                </div>
133	              )}
134	            </div>
135	          )}
136	        </div>
137	      ) : (
138	        !guest.include &&
139	        !cancelled && (
140	          <div className="guest__hint type-label-sm">Will not receive a pass. Toggle on to invite.</div>
141	        )
142	      )}
143	    </div>
144	  );
145	}
146	
```

1	/**
2	 * Auth (background only). Mints a Google id_token (engine auth) + access_token
3	 * (Calendar read) via chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow against a Web OAuth
4	 * client, caches them in storage.session, and silently re-mints on expiry.
5	 *
6	 * The id_token's audience must be on the engine's AUXILIO_WORKSPACE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID
7	 * allow-list. Calendar-only scopes (no Gmail/Drive — CASA line).
8	 */
9	import { OAUTH_CLIENT_ID } from './config';
10	import { engine } from './engine';
11	import type { AuthStatus } from './types';
12	
13	const SCOPES = [
14	  'openid',
15	  'email',
16	  'profile',
17	  'https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.events.readonly',
18	].join(' ');
19	
20	const KEY = 'auxilio.tokens';
21	
22	/** Durable (storage.local) marker that the user has connected and intends to stay
23	 *  connected. Token bundles live in storage.session and are WIPED on every browser
24	 *  restart, so an absent bundle can't tell "never signed in" from "session cleared,
25	 *  silent renew due." This flag survives restarts: while it's set, a failed silent
26	 *  renew means auth has lapsed (surface a reconnect), not that the user is signed
27	 *  out. Cleared only on explicit sign-out. */
28	const CONNECTED_KEY = 'auxilio.connected';
29	
30	interface TokenBundle {
31	  idToken: string;
32	  accessToken: string;
33	  email?: string;
34	  expiresAt: number;
35	}
36	
37	function parseFragment(redirectUrl: string) {
38	  const params = new URLSearchParams(new URL(redirectUrl).hash.slice(1));
39	  return {
40	    accessToken: params.get('access_token') ?? '',
41	    idToken: params.get('id_token') ?? '',
42	    code: params.get('code') ?? '',
43	    expiresIn: Number(params.get('expires_in') ?? '3600'),
44	    error: params.get('error') ?? undefined,
45	  };
46	}
47	
48	function emailFromIdToken(idToken: string): string | undefined {
49	  try {
50	    const part = idToken.split('.')[1].replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/');
51	    return JSON.parse(atob(part)).email as string | undefined;
52	  } catch {
53	    return undefined;
54	  }
55	}
56	
57	async function readCache(): Promise<TokenBundle | null> {
58	  const r = await chrome.storage.session.get(KEY);
59	  return (r[KEY] as TokenBundle) ?? null;
60	}
61	
62	async function mint(interactive: boolean): Promise<{ bundle: TokenBundle; code?: string }> {
63	  if (!OAUTH_CLIENT_ID) {
64	    throw new Error(
65	      'OAuth client id missing. Set WXT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID in .env and rebuild.',
66	    );
67	  }
68	  const url = new URL('https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth');
69	  url.searchParams.set('client_id', OAUTH_CLIENT_ID);
70	  // Interactive: hybrid flow (+ offline) so we ALSO get a code to hand the engine
71	  // for a calendar refresh token. Silent renew stays implicit (client tokens only).
72	  url.searchParams.set('response_type', interactive ? 'code token id_token' : 'token id_token');
73	  url.searchParams.set('redirect_uri', chrome.identity.getRedirectURL());
74	  url.searchParams.set('scope', SCOPES);
75	  url.searchParams.set('nonce', crypto.randomUUID());
76	  url.searchParams.set('prompt', interactive ? 'consent select_account' : 'none');
77	  if (interactive) url.searchParams.set('access_type', 'offline');
78	
79	  const redirect = await chrome.identity.launchWebAuthFlow({
80	    url: url.toString(),
81	    interactive,
82	  });
83	  if (!redirect) throw new Error('Sign-in was cancelled');
84	
85	  const { accessToken, idToken, code, expiresIn, error } = parseFragment(redirect);
86	  if (error) throw new Error(`Google sign-in error: ${error}`);
87	  if (!idToken || !accessToken) throw new Error('Sign-in did not return tokens');
88	
89	  const bundle: TokenBundle = {
90	    idToken,
91	    accessToken,
92	    email: emailFromIdToken(idToken),
93	    expiresAt: Date.now() + (expiresIn - 60) * 1000, // refresh 60s early
94	  };
95	  await chrome.storage.session.set({ [KEY]: bundle });
96	  return { bundle, code: code || undefined };
97	}
98	
99	/** Valid tokens, silently re-minting if expired. Throws if interactive sign-in
100	 *  is required (caller surfaces a sign-in gate). */
101	export async function getValidTokens(): Promise<TokenBundle> {
102	  const cached = await readCache();
103	  if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) return cached;
104	  return (await mint(false)).bundle; // prompt=none silent renew
105	}
106	
107	export async function signIn(): Promise<AuthStatus> {
108	  const { bundle, code } = await mint(true);
109	  // Mark "connected" durably so a later silent-renew failure reads as a lapse to
110	  // recover from, not a sign-out (see CONNECTED_KEY).
111	  await chrome.storage.local.set({ [CONNECTED_KEY]: true });
112	  // Best-effort: hand the auth code to the engine so it can mint a calendar
113	  // refresh token and keep passes in sync when the panel is closed. Never let a
114	  // failure here break sign-in — the engine's connectUrl CTA remains the fallback.
115	  if (code) {
116	    await engine
117	      .connectCalendar(bundle.idToken, code, chrome.identity.getRedirectURL())
118	      .catch((err) => console.warn('[auxilio] calendar connect failed', err));
119	  }
120	  return { signedIn: true, email: bundle.email };
121	}
122	
123	export async function signOut(): Promise<void> {
124	  const cached = await readCache();
125	  if (cached?.accessToken) {
126	    await fetch(
127	      `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke?token=${cached.accessToken}`,
128	      { method: 'POST' },
129	    ).catch(() => {});
130	  }
131	  await chrome.storage.session.remove(KEY);
132	  await chrome.storage.local.remove(CONNECTED_KEY);
133	}
134	
135	export async function authStatus(): Promise<AuthStatus> {
136	  const cached = await readCache();
137	  if (cached && cached.expiresAt > Date.now()) {
138	    return { signedIn: true, email: cached.email };
139	  }
140	  return { signedIn: false };
141	}
142	
143	/** Did the user connect at least once and not explicitly sign out? Survives the
144	 *  restart that wipes the storage.session token cache — so the background can tell
145	 *  a recoverable auth lapse from a genuine signed-out state. */
146	export async function wasConnected(): Promise<boolean> {
147	  const r = await chrome.storage.local.get(CONNECTED_KEY);
148	  return !!r[CONNECTED_KEY];
149	}
150	
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Calendar read (background only). Two jobs:
3	 *  1) resolve the canonical event for a given eid (events.get) — authoritative
4	 *     iCalUID + attendees for the side panel.
5	 *  2) list changes via a sync token (events.list) — the durable "what changed"
6	 *     API that drives post-save refresh + the visitor-event nudge/badge.
7	 * Calendar.events.readonly only.
8	 */
9	import { MAGIC_ADDRESS } from './config';
10	import type { ActiveEvent } from './types';
11	
12	/** `eid` is URL-safe base64 of "<eventId> <calendarId>" (calendarId optional). */
13	export function decodeEid(eid: string): { eventId: string; calendarId: string } | null {
14	  try {
15	    let b64 = eid.replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/');
16	    b64 += '='.repeat((4 - (b64.length % 4)) % 4);
17	    const decoded = atob(b64);
18	    const [eventId, calendarId] = decoded.split(' ');
19	    if (!eventId) return null;
20	    return { eventId, calendarId: calendarId || 'primary' };
21	  } catch {
22	    return null;
23	  }
24	}
25	
26	/** Inverse of decodeEid — build an eid the side panel can resolve. */
27	export function encodeEid(eventId: string, calendarId = 'primary'): string {
28	  const b64 = btoa(`${eventId} ${calendarId}`);
29	  return b64.replace(/\+/g, '-').replace(/\//g, '_').replace(/=+$/, '');
30	}
31	
32	export interface GCalEvent {
33	  id?: string;
34	  iCalUID?: string;
35	  status?: string;
36	  summary?: string;
37	  location?: string;
38	  description?: string;
39	  /** Present on instances of a recurring series (with singleEvents=true): the id
40	   *  of the parent series. Lets us treat a series as one nudge unit. */
41	  recurringEventId?: string;
42	  start?: { dateTime?: string; date?: string };
43	  end?: { dateTime?: string; date?: string };
44	  organizer?: { email?: string };
45	  attendees?: {
46	    email?: string;
47	    displayName?: string;
48	    resource?: boolean;
49	    self?: boolean;
50	    organizer?: boolean;
51	  }[];
52	}
53	
54	/** Is this a visitor event? (magic address among attendees or in the location.) */
55	export function isMarked(ev: GCalEvent): boolean {
56	  if (!MAGIC_ADDRESS) return false;
57	  const inAttendees = (ev.attendees ?? []).some(
58	    (a) => a.email?.toLowerCase() === MAGIC_ADDRESS,
59	  );
60	  const inLocation = (ev.location ?? '').toLowerCase().includes(MAGIC_ADDRESS);
61	  return inAttendees || inLocation;
62	}
63	
64	function startOf(ev: GCalEvent): string | undefined {
65	  return ev.start?.dateTime ?? ev.start?.date;
66	}
67	
68	async function getEvent(
69	  calendarId: string,
70	  eventId: string,
71	  accessToken: string,
72	): Promise<Response> {
73	  const url = new URL(
74	    `https://www.googleapis.com/calendar/v3/calendars/${encodeURIComponent(
75	      calendarId,
76	    )}/events/${encodeURIComponent(eventId)}`,
77	  );
78	  url.searchParams.set(
79	    'fields',
80	    'iCalUID,summary,location,description,start,end,organizer,attendees(email,displayName,resource,self,organizer)',
81	  );
82	  return fetch(url, { headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` } });
83	}
84	
85	export async function fetchActiveEvent(
86	  eid: string,
87	  accessToken: string,
88	): Promise<ActiveEvent> {
89	  const dec = decodeEid(eid);
90	  if (!dec) throw new Error('Could not read the event id from this page.');
91	
92	  // Recurring chips carry an instance id ("<base>_<ts>"); events.get often 404s
93	  // on the instance, so also try the base id. And try `primary` in case the
94	  // decoded calendar id isn't directly fetchable.
95	  const baseId = dec.eventId.includes('_') ? dec.eventId.split('_')[0] : null;
96	  const candidates: { cal: string; id: string }[] = [
97	    { cal: dec.calendarId, id: dec.eventId },
98	    ...(dec.calendarId !== 'primary' ? [{ cal: 'primary', id: dec.eventId }] : []),
99	    ...(baseId ? [{ cal: dec.calendarId, id: baseId }] : []),
100	    ...(baseId && dec.calendarId !== 'primary' ? [{ cal: 'primary', id: baseId }] : []),
101	  ];
102	  console.log('[auxilio] resolve event', { eid, ...dec, candidates });
103	
104	  let res: Response | null = null;
105	  let lastStatus = 0;
106	  let lastBody = '';
107	  for (const c of candidates) {
108	    const r = await getEvent(c.cal, c.id, accessToken);
109	    if (r.ok) {
110	      res = r;
111	      break;
112	    }
113	    lastStatus = r.status;
114	    lastBody = await r.text().catch(() => '');
115	    console.warn('[auxilio] events.get miss', c, r.status, lastBody.slice(0, 200));
116	  }
117	
118	  if (!res) {
119	    console.warn('[auxilio] resolve 404', { ...dec, lastBody: lastBody.slice(0, 200) });
120	    if (lastStatus === 404) {
121	      // Most common cause: a brand-new event that hasn't been saved yet — it
122	      // doesn't exist on Google's servers, so there's nothing to fetch.
123	      throw new Error('NOT_SAVED');
124	    }
125	    throw new Error(`Calendar API error (${lastStatus}). ${lastBody.slice(0, 120)}`);
126	  }
127	  const ev = (await res.json()) as GCalEvent;
128	  console.log('[auxilio] resolved event', {
129	    iCalUID: ev.iCalUID,
130	    summary: ev.summary,
131	    attendees: ev.attendees?.length ?? 0,
132	  });
133	
134	  const attendees = (ev.attendees ?? [])
135	    .filter((a) => a.email && !a.resource && a.email.toLowerCase() !== MAGIC_ADDRESS)
136	    .map((a) => ({ email: a.email as string, name: a.displayName }));
137	
138	  const rooms = (ev.attendees ?? [])
139	    .filter((a) => a.resource)
140	    .map((a) => a.displayName || a.email || '')
141	    .filter(Boolean);
142	
143	  return {
144	    iCalUid: ev.iCalUID ?? '',
145	    providerEventId: dec.eventId,
146	    title: ev.summary,
147	    start: startOf(ev),
148	    end: ev.end?.dateTime ?? ev.end?.date,
149	    location: ev.location,
150	    description: ev.description,
151	    rooms,
152	    organizerEmail: ev.organizer?.email,
153	    attendees,
154	  };
155	}
156	
157	/**
158	 * List changes on the primary calendar. With a sync token → only what changed
159	 * since last sync; without → an initial window of upcoming events (and a fresh
160	 * token). Paginates to capture nextSyncToken. Returns expired:true on a 410 so
161	 * the caller can full-resync.
162	 *
163	 * KNOWN LIMITATION (deferred by decision): primary calendar only. A visitor event
164	 * a host creates on a *secondary* calendar they own gets no sync-driven nudge
165	 * (badge/notification/banner) — the optimistic DOM path still fires if they open
166	 * it. Covering owned secondaries needs the calendar.calendarlist.readonly scope
167	 * (to discover them via calendarList.list) plus a per-calendar sync token. We
168	 * deferred this: the gap is unobserved and the extra scope/poll cost isn't worth
169	 * a maybe. Revisit only on a real report of a missed secondary-calendar event,
170	 * and when doing so, hard-filter to accessRole=owner (never poll subscribed/
171	 * holiday calendars).
172	 */
173	export async function listEvents(
174	  accessToken: string,
175	  opts: { syncToken?: string } = {},
176	): Promise<{ items: GCalEvent[]; nextSyncToken?: string; expired?: boolean }> {
177	  const items: GCalEvent[] = [];
178	  let pageToken: string | undefined;
179	  let nextSyncToken: string | undefined;
180	
181	  do {
182	    const url = new URL(
183	      'https://www.googleapis.com/calendar/v3/calendars/primary/events',
184	    );
185	    url.searchParams.set('singleEvents', 'true');
186	    url.searchParams.set('showDeleted', 'true');
187	    url.searchParams.set('maxResults', '250');
188	    url.searchParams.set(
189	      'fields',
190	      'items(id,iCalUID,status,summary,location,recurringEventId,start,end,attendees(email,resource)),nextPageToken,nextSyncToken',
191	    );
192	    if (opts.syncToken) {
193	      url.searchParams.set('syncToken', opts.syncToken);
194	    } else {
195	      // Initial sync: a forward window keeps the token small and relevant.
196	      url.searchParams.set('timeMin', new Date().toISOString());
197	    }
198	    if (pageToken) url.searchParams.set('pageToken', pageToken);
199	
200	    const res = await fetch(url, {
201	      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },
202	    });
203	    if (res.status === 410) return { items: [], expired: true }; // sync token stale
204	    if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Calendar list error (${res.status}).`);
205	    const data = (await res.json()) as {
206	      items?: GCalEvent[];
207	      nextPageToken?: string;
208	      nextSyncToken?: string;
209	    };
210	    if (data.items) items.push(...data.items);
211	    pageToken = data.nextPageToken;
212	    nextSyncToken = data.nextSyncToken ?? nextSyncToken;
213	  } while (pageToken);
214	
215	  return { items, nextSyncToken };
216	}
217	
```

> AGENT

I've now read the content script (button injection + fallback), the guest card (`RosterRow`), the OAuth/auth layer, and the Calendar client. Here are grounded answers to all four, no code changes yet.

---

## 1. People API for name + profile photo — my recommendation: **don't**

It conflicts with our hard rule #1 ("Calendar-only scopes. Never add Gmail/Drive… same hard line") and, more importantly, it wouldn't actually buy us much:

- **The subjects here are *external visitors*.** People API's directory lookup (`people.searchDirectoryPeople`, photos included) only resolves people **inside your own Workspace org**. External visitor emails — the whole point of this product — return nothing. So photos would appear for internal colleagues and never for the actual visitors.
- **It needs a new sensitive scope.** Resolving arbitrary attendees by email means `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/directory.readonly` (org directory) or `contacts.readonly`. Both are *sensitive* scopes → they widen our OAuth verification surface. We're "internal-first, no verification yet" precisely to avoid this; adding directory scope is a one-way door toward the verification exercise we deliberately deferred.
- **Names we already have, for free.** `fetchActiveEvent` already pulls `attendee.displayName` from `events.get` (`lib/calendar.ts:135-136`) — Calendar gives us the name Google knows with zero extra scope. Photos are the only thing missing, and that's the part People API can't deliver for visitors anyway.

So the honest cost/benefit: a sensitive scope + verification risk, to show avatars only for the minority of guests who are internal. Our **monogram avatar** (`RosterRow.tsx:14`) already gives every guest — internal or external — a stable, recognizable initial-based avatar. I'd keep it.

If you still want real photos for *internal* colleagues specifically, the cheaper path is to let the **engine** resolve them server-side (it can hold a Workspace Directory/Admin SDK credential for our own org once, centrally) and return a `photoUrl` on the draft roster — keeping the extension Calendar-only. That's a "later, if asked" engine change, not an extension scope change.

## 2. Dynamic dropdown label on guest cards — **yes, easy and self-contained**

Right now `RosterRow.tsx:108` is binary on phone only:
```
editing ? 'Hide details' : guest.phone ? 'Edit details' : 'Add phone & name'
```

I'll make it consider **both** name and phone (four real combinations, plain English per the no-dashes rule):

| name | phone | label |
|------|-------|-------|
| ✗ | ✗ | **Add phone and name** |
| ✓ | ✗ | **Add phone** |
| ✗ | ✓ | **Add name** |
| ✓ | ✓ | **Edit details** |

One nuance to lock: "name present" should mean a *real* name, not the email fallback. `display` falls back to `guest.email` when `guest.name` is empty, so I'll test `guest.name?.trim()` (the actual field), treating empty/whitespace as missing. Pure label logic — no data or engine changes.

## 3. Inline "Manage Visitors" button next to More options / Save — **yes, by reordering the editor anchor**

The hook already exists. In `findAnchor()` (`calendar.content.ts:440-457`), the editor case currently prefers a **full-width row** below Availability/Visibility, and only falls back to an **inline button before Save** (`buildInlineButton`, line 787) as a last resort. The inline path you're describing is built and working — it's just deprioritized.

The plan: for `/eventedit` (and the quick-create popover that has More options + Save), **promote the inline-next-to-Save placement to primary**, so it sits in the action bar and reads as a native inline button. I'll find the action bar by locating the Save button via `findButtonByText('save')` (already done at line 454) and insert our inline button beside it. Order becomes:

1. **Inline next to Save / More options** (primary — the inline feel you want)
2. Full-width row below Availability/Visibility (fallback, keeps it visible if the action bar can't be found)
3. Floating FAB (last resort — see #4)

`shieldInteractions` (line 719) already stops our button's clicks from triggering Google's Save, so dropping it into the action bar is safe. One thing to verify live: Google's editor action bar is a tight flex row, so I'll confirm the button doesn't wrap on a narrow window — may need a slightly more compact inline variant.

## 4. Graceful fallback button — **yes, still intact**

The floating fallback is fully present and wired: `mountFloating()` (`calendar.content.ts:809`) builds a shadow-DOM FAB, and `update()` (line 500-523) shows it **only after a short grace period when no native anchor injects** (`fab.setVisible(allowFab && !placed && attempts >= 3)`). It's gated by `allowFab`, which callers pass `false` when the side panel is already open (redundant then). So if our chosen inline/row spot isn't found on either the detail popover or the edit screen, the FAB still appears as the last-resort entry point. Reordering the anchors in #3 doesn't touch this — the FAB only fires when *all* native anchors fail.

---

**Summary of what's safe and self-contained vs. needs care:**
- #2 (dynamic label) — trivial, pure UI.
- #3 (inline placement) — moderate; reorder anchors + verify no wrap in the live editor.
- #4 — nothing to do, already works; I just confirmed it.
- #1 (People API) — I'd skip on scope/verification grounds; names already come from Calendar, and the only real-photo path I'd endorse is engine-side for internal colleagues.

Which one do you want to start with? I'd suggest #2 first (lowest risk), then #3.

1	/**
2	 * Resolve a guest's real name from the host's Google Contacts via the People API.
3	 *
4	 * Why: Google omits `attendee.displayName` for external non-contact guests — the
5	 * name shown in the Calendar UI is resolved client-side from the host's contacts
6	 * and never reaches the event API. This replicates that lookup server-side: given
7	 * the host's OAuth client, search their contacts (and "other contacts" they've
8	 * interacted with) for the attendee email and return the saved name.
9	 *
10	 * Requires contacts.readonly + contacts.other.readonly scopes (host must reconnect
11	 * to grant them) and the People API enabled in the GCP project. Only works when the
12	 * email is actually a saved/interacted contact — fails gracefully otherwise.
13	 */
14	
15	import { google } from 'googleapis';
16	
17	type PeopleAuth = InstanceType<typeof google.auth.OAuth2>;
18	
19	// Process-level caches. The search APIs need a one-time "warmup" (empty query)
20	// before they return results, so we prime each client once.
21	const warmedClients = new WeakSet<object>();
22	const nameCache = new Map<string, string | null>(); // email → name (null = looked up, not found)
23	
24	export async function resolveNameFromContacts(auth: PeopleAuth, email: string): Promise<string | undefined> {
25	  const key = email.toLowerCase();
26	  const cached = nameCache.get(key);
27	  if (cached !== undefined) return cached ?? undefined;
28	
29	  const people = google.people({ version: 'v1', auth });
30	
31	  if (!warmedClients.has(auth)) {
32	    warmedClients.add(auth);
33	    // Warmup — Google requires a prior request to prime the search cache.
34	    await people.people.searchContacts({ query: '', readMask: 'names,emailAddresses' }).catch(() => {});
35	    await people.otherContacts.search({ query: '', readMask: 'names,emailAddresses' }).catch(() => {});
36	  }
37	
38	  const name =
39	    (await searchContacts(people, email)) ??
40	    (await searchOtherContacts(people, email));
41	
42	  nameCache.set(key, name ?? null);
43	  return name;
44	}
45	
46	function pickName(person: { names?: { displayName?: string | null }[] | null; emailAddresses?: { value?: string | null }[] | null } | undefined, email: string): string | undefined {
47	  if (!person) return undefined;
48	  const emails = person.emailAddresses ?? [];
49	  if (!emails.some((e) => e.value?.toLowerCase() === email.toLowerCase())) return undefined;
50	  return person.names?.[0]?.displayName ?? undefined;
51	}
52	
53	async function searchContacts(people: ReturnType<typeof google.people>, email: string): Promise<string | undefined> {
54	  try {
55	    const res = await people.people.searchContacts({ query: email, readMask: 'names,emailAddresses', pageSize: 10 });
56	    for (const r of res.data.results ?? []) {
57	      const name = pickName(r.person ?? undefined, email);
58	      if (name) return name;
59	    }
60	  } catch (err: any) {
61	    console.warn(`[CalendarContacts] searchContacts failed for ${email}:`, err?.response?.data?.error?.message ?? err?.message ?? err);
62	  }
63	  return undefined;
64	}
65	
66	async function searchOtherContacts(people: ReturnType<typeof google.people>, email: string): Promise<string | undefined> {
67	  try {
68	    const res = await people.otherContacts.search({ query: email, readMask: 'names,emailAddresses', pageSize: 10 });
69	    for (const r of res.data.results ?? []) {
70	      const name = pickName(r.person ?? undefined, email);
71	      if (name) return name;
72	    }
73	  } catch (err: any) {
74	    console.warn(`[CalendarContacts] otherContacts.search failed for ${email}:`, err?.response?.data?.error?.message ?? err?.message ?? err);
75	  }
76	  return undefined;
77	}
78

> AGENT

You're right, and I need to correct what I told you. I was wrong that names come from Calendar "for free" — they don't, for external guests. Here's what's actually happening, confirmed in the code.

## Why Calendar shows "Junaid Ahmad" but we show the email

Google's own engine comment says it outright (`calendar-contacts.ts:4-8`):

> *"Google omits `attendee.displayName` for external non-contact guests — the name shown in the Calendar UI is resolved client-side from the host's contacts and never reaches the event API."*

So:
- For `j7654894110@gmail.com`, the Calendar **API** (`events.get` attendees) returns **no `displayName`**. The "Junaid Ahmad" you see in Google's guest list is resolved by Calendar's **web UI** from *your* Google Contacts / "other contacts" — it's never in the API payload we read.
- Our extension gets nothing, so the engine falls back to `nameFromEmail()` (`visit-drafts.ts:117`) — the email local-part, prettified. That's your `j7654894110`.

My earlier "names already come from Calendar" only holds for two cases: attendees the organizer explicitly named, and (sometimes) same-org directory members. External Gmail guests — the common visitor case — fall through. I shouldn't have generalized.

## The twist: the engine *already* has the fix, but it's switched off

There's `resolveNameFromContacts()` in `calendar-contacts.ts` that replicates exactly what Calendar's UI does — People API `searchContacts` + `otherContacts.search` on the host's account. That's precisely how "Junaid Ahmad" gets resolved (that email is in your contacts / you've interacted with it).

**But it has zero callers** (`grep` confirms) and it needs `contacts.readonly` + `contacts.other.readonly` — and per your own prior decision (the *calendar flow consolidation* note: *"Drop contacts scope"*) those scopes were deliberately dropped. The extension's token and the engine's calendar-connect token both carry only `calendar.events.readonly`, so even the dead code couldn't run on this path.

So this reframes Q1. The relevant People API scope is **not** `directory.readonly` (that's org-directory only — wouldn't have found an external Gmail; it's currently used only for employee sync). It's **contacts** scopes — and the engine is already built for it.

## Corrected options for getting the real name (and photo)

| Option | Gets "Junaid Ahmad"? | Photo? | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Do nothing** (current) | No | No (monogram) | Free. Host can type the real name; email is always shown. |
| **B. Wire up the existing engine contacts resolver** | Yes — when the email is in the host's contacts / other-contacts | Yes (add `photos` to readMask) | Re-add `contacts.readonly` + `contacts.other.readonly`; reverses the "drop contacts scope" decision; expands the eventual verification ask. Engine code already exists; mostly scope + one call-site wiring. |
| **C. Prettify-only** | No, but stops showing a phone-number-looking "name" as if it were one | No | Free. e.g. when there's no real name, lead with the email and don't present the local-part as a name. |

**Important caveat on B:** contacts (`contacts.*`) is a **sensitive** scope, *not* restricted — so it does **not** trigger CASA (that's still only Gmail/Drive). It doesn't violate the "never add Gmail/Drive" hard line. But it *does* reverse a decision you made on purpose, and it only resolves emails that are actually in the host's contacts — a genuinely unknown external email still won't resolve. The name is also cosmetic: the email is always displayed, and the host can correct the name inline.

## My recommendation

Given it's a values/scope-reversal call that's yours, not mine: I'd lean **C now, B only if the missing names are actually causing confusion at reception**. The engine machinery for B is already written, so it stays cheap to turn on later — which is the strongest argument for not rushing it.

Do you want to:
- **(B)** re-enable the contacts resolver + scope to match Google's names/photos, or
- **(C)** just stop showing the email local-part as a "name," or
- leave Q1 as-is and move on to the dynamic label (#2) and inline button (#3)?

I should also update my memory note here, since my "drop contacts scope" recollection just got confirmed as the live state — want me to record that the contacts resolver exists-but-unused so we don't re-derive this next time?
